In Table
1
we report the Mean-Squared-Error (MSE) for each column,
j=1,\ldots,r
, of the considered estimators, averaged over the
B
replications (with standard deviations in parenthesis):

\displaystyle\text{MSE}_{j}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left({\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny OLS}(b)}_{ij}-\bm{\Lambda}_{ij}\right)^{2}\right\},

\displaystyle\text{MSE}_{j}^{\text{\tiny PC}}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{(b)}_{ij}-\bm{\Lambda}_{ij}\right)^{2}\right\},\quad\text{MSE}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}(b)}_{ij}-\bm{\Lambda}_{ij}\right)^{2}\right\}.

Results show that QML and PC estimator have similar MSEs and both improve as
n
increases to the point that when
n=200
their MSEs are comparable with the one of the unfeasible OLS, i.e., the estimators behave as if the factors were observed. In most cases the QML estimator has a smaller MSE even when the true distribution is not Gaussian.

Finally, in Table
2
for each column of the loadings,
j=1,\ldots,r
, we report the distance between the QML and PC estimators measured as (with standard deviations in parenthesis):

\displaystyle\text{D}_{j}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}(b)}_{ij}-\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{ij}^{(b)}\right)^{2}\right\}.

And we also report the relative MSE of the PC estimator with respect to the MSE of the QML estimator:
\text{MSE}_{j}^{\text{\tiny REL}}={\text{MSE}_{j}^{\text{\tiny PC}}}/{\text{MSE}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}
. Results clearly show that as
n
grows the PC and QML estimators become almost indistinguishable.

9
Concluding remarks

To compute in practice the QML estimator of the loadings there are at least two main issues. First, in finite samples the QML estimator of the loadings has no closed form and depends also on the estimator of the idiosyncratic covariance for which no closed form exists either. This is true even if we use the the log-likelihood (
13
) of an exact factor model. Second, the convergence properties of the various available EM algorithms used to compute the QML estimator
(
Rubin and
Thayer 1982
;
Bai and Li 2012
;
Bai and Li 2016
;
Zadrozny 2023
)
have never been fully investigated. On the one hand, it is easy to prove, that at each iteration of an EM algorithm the log-likelihood evaluated in the parameters estimated at that iteration is larger than at the previous iteration
(
Wu 1983
)
, but, on the other hand, no formal proof exists of convergence of those algorithms to a global maximum of the likelihood, at least to the best of our knowledge.

The results of this paper offer a possible solution by showing that, if we are just interested in the factor loadings and we do not need to estimate the idiosyncratic variances, then we can simply use the PC estimator of the loadings and of its asymptotic covariance matrix to approximate the corresponding QML estimator and its asymptotic covariance matrix. Once this is done, the factors can be estimated via OLS as in PC analysis.

As a consequence, we might think that there is no apparent advantage in directly computing the QML estimator of the loadings and of the idiosyncratic variances.

Nevertheless, QML estimation has at least three advantages. First, it allows us to easily impose restrictions on the parameters of the model. Second, having also the QML estimator of the idiosyncratic variances allows us to compute estimators of the factors as the GLS, which are possibly more efficient. Third, QML estimation, as presented in this paper, is a first step towards estimating a model where we explicitly model the dynamics of the factors, something we cannot do with PC analysis. This last point, already briefly discussed in Remarks
4
and
7
, is the subject of our ongoing research
(
Barigozzi and
Luciani 2024
)
.

Appendix A
Proof of main results

A.1
Proof of Theorem
1

From (
7
) and (
8
), and since by (
9
) we have
\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}=\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}=\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x})^{-1/2}
which is well defined because of Lemma
8
(iv), we get

\frac{\bm{X}^{\prime}\bm{X}}{nT}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}=\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}.

(A.1)

Then, substituting
\bm{X}^{\prime}\bm{X}=(\bm{\Lambda}\bm{F}^{\prime}+\bm{\Xi}^{\prime})^{\prime}(\bm{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\bm{\Xi})
into (
A.1
)

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}=\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}.

(A.2)

Define

\widehat{\mathbf{H}}=\left(\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}=\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1},

(A.3)

by Assumption
5
(b). Notice that, as
n,T\to\infty
,
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
is well defined because
\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}
is well defined because of Lemma
8
(iv). From (
A.2
) and (
A.3
)

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}

\displaystyle-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}=\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}{\bm{\Lambda}}}{nT}\right)\bm{\mathcal{H}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\!\!\!\!\!+\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}}{nT}+\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right)(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}})\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}.

(A.4)

Taking the
i
th row of (
A.4
)

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}=

\displaystyle\,\left(\underbrace{\frac{1}{nT}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}}_{\text{(1.a)}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bm{\lambda}_{j}\bm{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}}_{\text{(1.b)}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}\bm{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}}_{\text{(1.c)}}\right)\bm{\mathcal{H}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle+\left(\underbrace{\frac{1}{nT}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}-\bm{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\mathcal{H}})}_{\text{(1.d)}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bm{\lambda}_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}-\bm{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\mathcal{H}})}_{\text{(1.e)}}\right.

\displaystyle\;\;\left.+\underbrace{\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}-\bm{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\mathcal{H}})}_{\text{(1.f)}}\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}.

(A.5)

From Proposition
B.3
we see that, under Assumptions
1
through
4
, the terms
(1.a)
,
(1.c)
,
(1.d)
,
(1.e)
, and
(1.f)
are all
o_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
. In particular, from (
A.5
), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right)\right).

(A.6)

Consistency follows immediately since the first term in (
A.6
) is
O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
because of Assumption
7
(see also (
B.42
) in the proof of Theorem
5
) and since
\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
because of Lemma
8
(iv) and
11
.

Then, from (
A.6
),
by Proposition
B.4
(b):

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}\right)+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}\right)+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.7)

Now, from Proposition
B.6
we have that, as
n,T\to\infty
,

\min(\sqrt{n},\sqrt{T})\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}-\bm{J}\|=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
, and, by using the definition of
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
in (
A.3
), it follows that (
A.1
) is equivalent to (note that
\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|=O(1)
by Assumption
1
(a))

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-\bm{J}\bm{\lambda}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\bm{\lambda}_{i})+\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}-\bm{J})\bm{\lambda}_{i}+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}\right)+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}\right)+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\bm{J}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}\right)+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)

\displaystyle\to_{d}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0}_{r},\bm{\Phi}_{i}\right),

(A.8)

where we used Slutsky’s theorem and Assumption
7
. Notice that,
\bm{J}
plays no role in the covariance since it is diagonal and
\bm{J}^{2}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. Because of Assumption
6
the sign indeterminacy on the left-hand-side can be easily fixed so that
\bm{J}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. By substituting
\mathbf{I}_{r}
in place of
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
in (
A.6
) and using Assumption
7
it follows also that:

\left\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right).

This completes the proof of part (a).

For part (b), from (
A.4
)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}}{n^{3/2}T}\right\|\right)\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle+\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right\|\right)\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|,

and the proof of part (b) follows from
Proposition
B.2
and Lemma

2
(i),

3
,

4
(i),

4
(ii),

4
(iii),

8
(iv),

11
. This proves part (b) and completes the proof.

\Box

A.2
Proof of Corollary
1

From (
A.6
) and the second last line of (
A.1
) in the proof of Theorem
1
and by imposing the identification constraint of orthonormal factors in Assumption
5
(b) and
\bm{J}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
by Assumption
6
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-\bm{\lambda}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right)\right),

(A.9)

where the rate of the last term comes from (
A.6
) in the proof of Theorem
1
(a). By definition of OLS, and again imposing Assumption
5
(b), we have:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-\bm{\lambda}_{i}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}.

(A.10)

By comparing (
A.9
) and (
A.10
) we complete the proof of part (a). Part (b) follows also directly from Theorem
1
(b). This completes the proof.

\Box

A.3
Proof of Theorem
3

In principle, we could try to replicate the proofs by
Bai and Li 2016
under our identifying constraints and using only our assumptions. However, there is a much simpler and intuitive way to proceed.

Consider the log-likelihood (
12
). The parameters to be estimated are given by
{\bm{\varphi}}=(\mathrm{vec}({\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime},\mathrm{vech}({\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi})^{\prime})^{\prime}
. Let also
\widehat{\bm{\varphi}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=(\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}})^{\prime},\mathrm{vech}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}})^{\prime})^{\prime}
denote the maximizer of (
12
) and

\underline{\bm{\varphi}}=(\mathrm{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime},\mathrm{vech}(\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi})^{\prime})^{\prime}
denote a generic value of the parameters.
Whenever we consider
\underline{\bm{\varphi}}
it is intended that its elements satisfy Assumptions
1
through
6
.

Then, the elements of
\widehat{\bm{\varphi}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
are such that:

\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}=\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x},

(A.11)

where
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}=T^{-1}{\bm{X}^{\prime}\bm{X}}
. To see that (
A.11
) defines the global maximum of the log-likelihood we proceed in two steps.

First, notice that the first order conditions derived from the log-likelihood (
12
) are satisfied when (
A.11
) holds:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\ell(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}=\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}

\displaystyle=T\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-T\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}

\displaystyle=T\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-T\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=\mathbf{0}_{r\times n},

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\ell(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}=\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{2}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}-\frac{T}{2}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{2}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}-\frac{T}{2}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}=\mathbf{0}_{n\times n}.

Notice also that the conditions given in
Bai and Li 2012
, which are derived from the first order conditions above, are also satisfied. Namely, it holds that:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\left\{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right\}=\mathbf{0}_{r\times n},

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}=\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}.

Second, given the log-likelihood (
12
), for any
\underline{\bm{\varphi}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\ell(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widehat{\bm{\varphi}}^{\text{\tiny QML}})

\displaystyle-\ell(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{T}{2}\log\frac{\det\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)}{\det\left(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)}-\frac{nT}{2}+\frac{T}{2}\text{tr}\left\{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\left(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)^{-1}\right\}

(A.12)

\displaystyle=-\frac{T}{2}\log\frac{\det\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)}{\det\left(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)}-\frac{nT}{2}+\frac{T}{2}\text{tr}\left\{\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)\left(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)^{-1}\right\},

because of (
A.11
). Now, denote as
\zeta_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,n
, the
n
roots of

\det\left\{\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)-\zeta\left(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)\right\}=0,

which are all real since
\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)\left(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)^{-1}
is a symmetric matrix. Then, (
A.12
) reads:

\ell(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widehat{\bm{\varphi}}^{\text{\tiny QML}})-\ell(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})=\frac{T}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\{-\log\zeta_{j}-1+\zeta_{j}\right\}\geq 0,

(A.13)

since
x\leq e^{x-1}
and so
-\log x-1+x\geq 0
. Therefore, from (
A.13
) we see that (
A.11
) defines indeed the global maximum of the log-likleihood (
12
).

Now, consider the Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) of the true loadings:

\frac{{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}}={\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{U}},

(A.14)

where
{\mathbf{V}}
is
n\times r
and such that
{\mathbf{V}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{V}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

{\mathbf{D}}
is
r\times r
diagonal with strictly positive entries, and
{\mathbf{U}}
is
r\times r
such that

{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}={\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{U}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
.

We know that under Assumption
5
and
6
,
\bm{\Lambda}
is globally identified. Let us show that
{\mathbf{V}}
,
{\mathbf{D}}
, and
{\mathbf{U}}
in (
A.14
) are also globally identified. First, notice that, given Assumption
5
(a) which requires
n^{-1}{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}}
to be diagonal, in order to estimate
{\bm{\Lambda}}
we need to estimate
nr-{r(r-1)}/{2}
parameters. Then, by looking at the
right-hand-side of (
A.14
) we see that to estimate
{\mathbf{V}}
we need to estimate
nr-\frac{r(r+1)}{2}
parameters and to estimate
{\mathbf{D}}
we need to estimate
r
parameters, thus
{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{D}}
depends on
nr-{r(r+1)}/{2}+r=nr-{r(r-1)}/{2}
parameters as
{\bm{\Lambda}}
. However, in principle the
right-hand-side of (
A.14
) depends also on
{\mathbf{U}}
which in turn requires estimating
{r(r+1)}/2
parameters more. But if we impose Assumption
5
(a) also to the right-hand-side of (
A.14
) we have that
n^{-1}{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{D}}^{2}{\mathbf{U}}
has to be diagonal, and since
{\mathbf{D}}
is diagonal, without loss of generality we can set
{\mathbf{U}}=\mathbf{J}
, a diagonal
r\times r
matrix with entries
\pm 1
.

Furthermore, from Proposition
B.1
(a) we also see that we must have

n^{-1}{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}}=n^{-1}{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}=\mathbf{D}^{2}
.
Hence,

\mathbf{D}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{1/2},

(A.15)

and by Lemma
1
(iv) the entries of
\mathbf{D}
, denoted as
d_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, are such that

\sqrt{\underline{C}_{j}}\!\leq\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}d_{j}\leq\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}d_{j}\leq\!\sqrt{\overline{C}_{j}},

(A.16)

where
\underline{C}_{j}
and
\overline{C}_{j}
are finite positive reals. Last, from (
A.14
) and Proposition
B.1
(b), we must have

n^{-1/2}{{\bm{\Lambda}}}={\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{J}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\left(n^{-1}{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}\right)^{1/2}
, and by (
A.15
) it follows that

{\mathbf{V}}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}.

(A.17)

This shows that under Assumption
5
(a), the parameters in
{\mathbf{D}}
are globally identified while the parameters in
\mathbf{V}
are uniquely identified up to a right-multiplication by
\mathbf{J}
, which can be pinned down by means of Assumption
6
, thus achieving global identification of
\mathbf{V}
as well. Given this discussion, hereafter, we can directly set
\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
.

It is clear that the problem of QML estimation of the loadings can be rewritten as a problem of QML estimation of their SVD in (
A.14
), namely of
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{D}
. To this end, we introduce also the SVDs of the QML estimator of the loadings and of a generic value of the loadings:

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}{\sqrt{n}}=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}^{\text{\tiny QML}},\hskip 18.49988pt\frac{\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}}=\underline{\mathbf{V}}\,\underline{\mathbf{D}}\,\underline{\mathbf{U}},

(A.18)

where

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
and
\underline{\mathbf{V}}
have the same properties as
{\mathbf{V}}
,

\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
and
\underline{\mathbf{D}}
have the same properties as
{\mathbf{D}}
,
and

\widehat{\mathbf{U}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
and
\underline{\mathbf{U}}
have the same properties as
{\mathbf{U}}
.

Because we set
\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, it follows that we can set
\widehat{\mathbf{U}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, and we are left with the task of finding
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
. From (
A.11
) and since we must have
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, it follows that

\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}}{n}-\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{2}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}-\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}}{n}=\mathbf{0}_{n\times n},

which is equivalent to

\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}{n}-\left(\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}{n}=\mathbf{0}_{r\times r}.

(A.19)

Now, let
\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, be the
j
th column of
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
and let

\widehat{d}_{j}^{\,\text{\tiny QML}}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, be the
j
th diagonal entry of
\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
. Then, from (
A.19
), for all
j=1,\ldots,r
, we have

\frac{\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}{n}-\left(\widehat{d}_{j}^{\,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}{n}=0.

(A.20)

Then, trivially, the QML estimators are such that:

\left(\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}},\widehat{d}_{j}^{\,\text{\tiny QML}},\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}\right)=\arg\!\!\!\!\min_{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j},\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}}\left(\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}-\underline{d}_{j}^{2}-\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}\right)^{2}=\arg\!\!\!\!\min_{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j},\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}}\mathcal{L}_{0j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j},\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right),\;\text{say,}

(A.21)

where
\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, is the
j
th column of
\underline{\mathbf{V}}
and

\underline{d}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, is the
j
th diagonal entry of
\underline{\mathbf{D}}
.

Define also the estimators
\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}
and
\widetilde{d}_{j}
such that:

\left(\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j},\widetilde{d}_{j}\right)=\arg\!\min_{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j}}\left(\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}-\underline{d}_{j}^{2}\right)^{2}=\arg\!\min_{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j}}\mathcal{L}_{1j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j}\right),\;\text{say.}

(A.22)

Consistently with (
A.14
) and (
A.18
), these define an estimator of
\bm{\Lambda}
by means of its SVD:

\frac{\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}},

(A.23)

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}
has columns
\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, and it has the same properties as
{\mathbf{V}}
(because
\underline{\mathbf{V}}
does), and

\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}
has entries
\widetilde{d}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, and it has the same properties as
{\mathbf{D}}
(because
\underline{\mathbf{D}}
does). We set
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
consistently with the fact that
\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
.

Then, it is easily seen that

\mathcal{L}_{0j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j},\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)=\mathcal{L}_{1j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j}\right)+\left(\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}\right)^{2}-2\left(\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{1j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j}\right)}.

(A.24)

Now, we know that for any generic value of the idiosyncratic covariance matrix satisfying Assumption
2
(b), it holds that

\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}\leq\max_{\bm{w}\,:\,\bm{w}^{\prime}\bm{w}=1}\frac{\bm{w}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\bm{w}}{n}=\frac{\underline{\mu}^{\xi}_{1}}{n}\leq\frac{M_{2\xi}}{n},

(A.25)

since
\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}=1
and because of Lemma
1
(v) which implies also that
M_{2\xi}
is independent of
n
. Moreover, for any generic
\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}
satisfying Assumption
1
(a):

\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{1j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j}\right)}=\left|\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}-\underline{d}_{j}^{2}\right|\leq\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}+\underline{d}_{j}^{2}\leq\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{x}}{n}+\underline{d}_{j}^{2}=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1),

(A.26)

since
\underline{d}_{j}=O(1)
because
\|n^{-1/2}{\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}}\|=O(1)
by Lemma
2
(i), and
\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{x}=O_{\mathrm{P}}(n)
because of Lemma
8
(iii). Therefore, from (
A.24
), (
A.25
), and (
A.26
), we have

\left|\mathcal{L}_{0j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j},\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right)-\mathcal{L}_{1j}\left(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j}\right)\right|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(A.27)

which holds for any
\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}
satisfying Assumption
2
(b). By continuity of these loss functions, from (
A.27
) it follows that their minima satisfy:

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\hskip 18.49988pt\left|\widehat{d}_{j}^{\,\text{\tiny QML}}-\widetilde{d}_{j}\right|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(A.28)

for all
j=1,\ldots,r
.

Let us now find
\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}
and
\widetilde{d}_{j}
. From (
A.22
), it is clear that the solutions must be such that:

\frac{\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}}{n}=\widetilde{d}_{j}^{\,2},

(A.29)

which means that
\widetilde{d}_{j}^{\,2}
must be an eigenvalue of
n^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}}
and
\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}
is the corresponding normalized eigenvector. Obviously, the solution in (
A.31
) defines a global minimum of the loss
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j})
since
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j},\widetilde{d}_{j})=0
while
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\underline{\bm{v}}_{j},\underline{d}_{j})>0
for any other value
\underline{\bm{v}}_{j}\neq\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}
and
\underline{d}_{j}\neq\widetilde{d}_{j}
.

Now, let us show that indeed it must be that
\widetilde{d}_{j}^{\,2}=n^{-1}{\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{x}}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
, i.e., they have to be the
r
largest eigenvalues of
n^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}}
. First, by Lemma
6
(i) and Weyl’s inequality, for all
k=1,\ldots,r
, as
n,T\to\infty
,

\left|\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{k}^{x}}{n}-\frac{{\mu}_{k}^{x}}{n}\right|\leq\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}}{n}-\frac{{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}}{n}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right).

(A.30)

Therefore, if for any given
j=1,\ldots,r
we were to choose
\widetilde{d}_{j}^{\,2}=n^{-1}{\widehat{\mu}_{k}^{x}}
for, say,
k=r+1
, then, from (
A.30
), we would have

\displaystyle\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\widetilde{d}_{j}^{\,2}=\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{r+1}^{x}}{n}=\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\frac{{\mu}_{r+1}^{x}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\geq\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\frac{{\mu}_{n}^{\xi}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),

\displaystyle\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}\widetilde{d}_{j}^{\,2}=\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{r+1}^{x}}{n}=\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}\frac{{\mu}_{r+1}^{x}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{M_{\xi}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),

since we assumed
\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}
to be positive definite, so
\mu^{\xi}_{n}>0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and by Lemma
1
(vi). Therefore, as
n,T\to\infty
, this choice for
\widetilde{d}_{j}
cannot be a consistent estimator of
d_{j}
(nor an approximation of
\widehat{d}_{j}^{\,\text{\tiny QML}}
), since while
\widetilde{d}_{j}\to 0
, as
n,T\to\infty
, it must be that
d_{j}>0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, as required in (
A.16
).

So from (
A.29
) and the above reasoning it follows that, for all
j=1,\ldots,r
,

\widetilde{d}_{j}^{\,2}=\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{x}}{n},\hskip 18.49988pt\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{j}=\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{x}.

(A.31)

where
\widehat{\bm{v}}_{j}^{x}
is the eigenvector of
n^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}}
corresponding to its
j
th largest eigenvalue.

Then, from (
A.31
), first we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\right\|=\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(A.32)

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\,\text{\tiny QML}}-\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\right\|=\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\,\text{\tiny QML}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(A.33)

because of (
A.28
), and, second, we have

\frac{\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}}=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}=\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}},

(A.34)

which shows that the estimator
\widetilde{\bm{\Lambda}}

is the PC estimator defined in (
9
). Therefore, from (
A.32
), (
A.33
), and (
A.34
), and by using the SVD of the QML estimator in (
A.18
), it follows that:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}{\sqrt{n}}-\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|=

\displaystyle\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\right\|+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

because
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\|=1
(it is a matrix of normalized eigenvectors) and
\|(n^{-1}{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}})^{1/2}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
by Lemma
8
(iii). This proves part (a).

Finally, consider the
r
dimensional
i
th rows,
i=1,\ldots,n
, of
{\mathbf{V}}^{\chi}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}
,
\underline{\mathbf{V}}
, and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
, denoted as
{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\chi\prime}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x\prime}
,
\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\prime}
, and
\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}
, respectively. From Lemma
7
(ii) and
7
(iii), we know that
\sqrt{n}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\chi\prime}\|=O(1)
and
\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x\prime}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
, which means that we must have
\sqrt{n}\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\prime}\|=O(1)
since any generic parameter we consider is assumed to satisfy the same assumptions as the corresponding true parameters (
{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\chi\prime}
in this case). Therefore, since the search of the elements of
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
is made over all
\underline{\mathbf{V}}
satisfying the same assumptions as
{\mathbf{V}}^{\chi}
, it follows that we must have also
\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
.

To see that this is the case, notice also that because of Assumption
5
(a) the QML estimator must be such that
n^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}
is diagonal and positive definite. Thus, we must have
\|n^{-1/2}{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
(see also Lemma
2
(i)). From the SVD in (
A.18
) when
\widehat{\mathbf{U}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
and since it must be that
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then it follows that
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
and
\|\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(\sqrt{n})
. Moreover, since by Assumption
1
(a) the
i
th row of

\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
must be such that
\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
and
\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}=\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}
, then it must be that
\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
.

From this reasoning and (
A.32
) it follows that:

\sqrt{n}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(A.35)

Now, by taking the
i
th row of the PC estimator in (
A.34
) and the SVD of the QML estimator in (
A.18
), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}-\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|=

\displaystyle\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}\prime}\sqrt{n}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x\prime}(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x})^{1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x}\right\|\,\left\|\left({\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}\right)^{1/2}\right\|+\left\|\sqrt{n}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\left({\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}\right)^{1/2}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x}\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x}\right\|\,\left\|\sqrt{n}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\left({\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}\right)^{1/2}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

because of (
A.33
), (
A.35
), and since
\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(n^{-1/2})
by Lemma
7
(ii) and
7
(iii), and
\|({\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}})^{1/2}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(\sqrt{n})
by Lemma
8
(iii). This proves part (b) and completes the proof.
\Box

A.4
Proof of Theorem
4

From Theorem
3
(b) and Corollary
1
it follows that:

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\|

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\|+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}\|+\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\|+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\|+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right),

(A.36)

since the unfeasible OLS estimator is
\sqrt{T}
-consistent. Moreover, if
\sqrt{T}/n\to 0
as
n,T\to\infty
,
by imposing the identification constraint of orthonormal factors in Assumption
5
(b),
we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML}}-\bm{\lambda}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-\bm{\lambda}_{i})+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{it}+o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)\to_{d}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0}_{r},\bm{\Phi}_{i}\right).

by Slutsky’s theorem and Assumption
7
. This completes the proof of part (a). Part (b) follows similarly from Theorem
3
(a). This completes the proof.

\Box

A.5
Proof of Corollary
2

The proof of part (a) follows by using the log-likelihood (
13
) of an exact factor model in place of the log-likelihood (
12
), then, by
noticing that
\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi}
is positive definite by Assumption
2
(a), and fnally by
replacing in the proof of Theorem
3
,
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}
and
\underline{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}
with
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi,\text{\tiny QML}}

and
\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi}
respectively. The proof of part (b) is the same but when using
\sigma^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n}
, with
\sigma^{2}>0
, in place of
\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi}
.
\Box

A.6
Proof of Theorem
5

First of all, denote the log-likelihoods for one observation:

\displaystyle\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})=-\frac{1}{2}\log\det(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{x}_{t}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{t},

(A.37)

\displaystyle\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t}|\mathbf{F}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})=-\frac{1}{2}\log\det(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{x}_{t}-\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\mathbf{F}_{t})^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}(\mathbf{x}_{t}-\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\mathbf{F}_{t}),

(A.38)

Let us consider part (a). For any fixed value of the parameters, say
\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}
, let

\displaystyle\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{s}_{1}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\vdots\\
\bm{s}_{n}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t}|\mathbf{F}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{s}_{1}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\vdots\\
\bm{s}_{n}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\end{array}\right),

which are
n\times r
matrices of first derivatives, and where, for any given
i=1,\ldots,n
,

\bm{s}_{i}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}},\hskip 9.24994pt\bm{s}_{i}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t}|\mathbf{F}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}},

(A.45)

which are
r
-dimensional column vectors.

Then, recalling that
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{t}\mathbf{x}_{t}^{\prime}
, by computing the first derivatives of the log-likelihood (
A.37
), we have

\displaystyle\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})=

\displaystyle\,-T(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}+(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-T(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}+T(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-T(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}+T(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1},

(A.46)

where we used the Woodbury identities

\displaystyle(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}=(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1},

(A.47)

\displaystyle(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}+\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}=(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}-(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}.

(A.48)

In what follows, we make use of the following results. Let
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}
and let
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi}=\text{diag}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi})
, the diagonal matrix having as diagonal entries the diagonal entries of
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}
. Then, by using twice Lemma
5
(ii) and by Lemma
1
(v), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi}-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi}-\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi}\right\|+\frac{1}{n}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}-\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi}\right\|\leq O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right\|+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(A.49)

which implies:

\left\|(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}-\mathbf{I}_{n}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right).

(A.50)

Moreover,

\left\|\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(A.51)

because of Lemma
13
,

\left\|\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{n\underline{C}_{r}\|({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\|}=\frac{1}{n\underline{C}_{r}\min_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sigma_{i}^{2}}=\frac{1}{n\underline{C}_{r}C_{\xi}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(A.52)

because of Lemma
1
(iv)-
1
(vi), Assumption
2
(a), and
Merikoski and
Kumar 2004
,

\displaystyle\left\|\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}-\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right\|=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),

(A.53)

because of (
A.51
) and (
A.52
),

\displaystyle\left\|\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\right\|=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(A.54)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Lambda}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}-\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right\|=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\right),

(A.55)

because of (
A.52
), (
A.53
) and since
\|{\bm{\Lambda}}\|=O(\sqrt{n})
by Lemma
2
(i), and, last,

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}-\bm{\Lambda}\right\|=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(A.56)

because of (
A.51
) and since
\|{\bm{\Lambda}}\|=O(\sqrt{n})
by Lemma
2
(i).

Now, denote
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}
and
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}=\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F\prime}
, so that we can write

\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}=\bm{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}+\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}=\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}+\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime},

(A.57)

because of Assumption
5
(b). Let us consider (
A.46
) when computed in the true value of the parameters. By means of (
A.50
)-(
A.55
) and (
A.57
) we have

\displaystyle\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};{\bm{\varphi}})=

\displaystyle\,T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\left\{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}+\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\right\}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\left\{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}+\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}+\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\right\}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+O\left(\frac{T}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle+T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+O\left(\frac{T}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}+T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle+T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{T}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O\left(\frac{T}{n^{3/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}

\displaystyle-T(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+O\left(\frac{T}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O\left(\frac{T}{n^{3/2}}\right).

(A.58)

Then, notice that
[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}]_{i\cdot}\bm{\xi}_{t}=[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}]_{ii}\xi_{it}=\frac{\xi_{it}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}
, and

[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}]_{i\cdot}\bm{\Lambda}=\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}
,
i=1,\ldots,n
.
The following holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(A.59)

\displaystyle T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right]_{i\cdot}=\frac{T}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{T}{n^{2}}\right),

(A.60)

\displaystyle T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right]_{i\cdot}=\frac{T}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{T}{n^{2}}\right),

(A.61)

\displaystyle T\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}=T\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+O\left(\frac{T}{n^{2}}\right),

(A.62)

\displaystyle T{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\left\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}=T\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}+O\left(\frac{T}{n}\right),

(A.63)

where we used Assumption
1
(a) and then (
A.59
) follows from (
A.52
), (
A.60
) and (
A.61
) follow from (
A.50
) and (
A.59
), (
A.62
) follows from (
A.53
), and (
A.63
) follows from and (
A.56
).

Therefore, by using (
A.60
)-(
A.63
) in (
A.58
) we have that the
i
th row of
\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})
is such that

\displaystyle\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\xi}_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}+O\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{T}{n^{2}}\right).

(A.64)

Hence,
\|\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(\sqrt{T})
, because of Assumption
2
(a), (
A.59
) and since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathbf{F}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{nT}\right\|^{2}\right]=O\left(\frac{1}{nT}\right)\;\text{ and }\;\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathbf{F}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|^{2}\right]=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),

(A.65)

because of (
B.19
) in the proof of Proposition
B.3
(a) and by Lemma
3
.
Moreover, from (
A.64
) and by noticing that
(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}
has the same properties as
{\bm{\Lambda}}
since
\|(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\|=O(1)
by Assumption
2
(a), we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})-\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\xi}_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{C_{\xi}}\left\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{T}}{C_{\xi}}\left\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right\|+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\sqrt{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\right)\right),

(A.66)

.

Finally, by computing the first derivatives of the log-likelihood (
A.38
), we have

\displaystyle\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\mathbf{x}_{t}-\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\mathbf{F}_{t})\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}.

(A.67)

And (
A.67
) when computed in the true value of the parameters is

\displaystyle\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}.

(A.68)

From (
A.68
), the
i
th row of
\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})
is then:

\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})=\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\xi}_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}.

(A.69)

Hence,
\|\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(\sqrt{T})
because of (
B.42
), and, by using (
A.69
) in (
A.66
), for any given
i=1,\ldots,n
we have:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})-\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\right)\right),

which proves part (a).

Turning to part (b). For any specific value of the parameters, say
\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}
, let

\displaystyle\bm{H}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left.\frac{\partial\text{vec}(\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}}))^{\prime}}{\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\bm{h}_{11}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})&\ldots&\bm{h}_{1n}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\bm{h}_{n1}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})&\ldots&\bm{h}_{nn}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\bm{H}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t}|\mathbf{F}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left.\frac{\partial\text{vec}(\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}}))^{\prime}}{\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\bm{h}_{11}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})&\ldots&\bm{h}_{1n}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\bm{h}_{n1}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})&\ldots&\bm{h}_{nn}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\\
\end{array}\right),

which are
nr\times nr
matrices obtained from the matricization of the 4th order tensor of second derivatives, and where, for any given
i=1,\ldots,n
,

\displaystyle\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left.\frac{\partial\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}},

(A.76)

\displaystyle\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\ell_{t}(\mathbf{x}_{t}|\mathbf{F}_{t};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}}=\left.\frac{\partial\bm{s}_{i}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})}{\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}}\right|_{\underline{\bm{\varphi}}={\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}}},

which are
r\times r
matrices.

We now use the following relation:

\displaystyle\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\bm{S}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\partial\text{vec}(\bm{S}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}}))^{\prime}}{\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}}\right)^{\prime}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime})=\bm{H}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}),

(A.77)

Then, by denoting
\underline{\bm{P}}=\left\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}
, from (
A.46
) we have

\displaystyle\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\bm{S}^{\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})\right)=

\displaystyle\,-T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle-T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right\}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle+T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right\}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle-T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle-T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right\}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle-T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right\}\bm{C}_{n,r}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle-T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle-T\left\{(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right\}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}).

(A.78)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right)\left\{\left[\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{r}\right]+\left[\mathbf{I}_{r}\otimes\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\right\}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-\left\{\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]+\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\right\}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}),

(A.79)

where
\bm{C}_{n,r}
is the
nr\times nr
commutation matrix such that
\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})=\bm{C}_{n,r}\text{vec}(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime})
.
Therefore, from (
A.77
), (
A.79
), and (
A.6
) we get

\displaystyle\bm{H}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})=

\displaystyle\,T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.1}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.2}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.3}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.4}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.5}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.6}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.7}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.8}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it B.1}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it B.2}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it B.3}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it B.4}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right].\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it B.5}

(A.80)

Let us consider (
A.6
) when computed in the true value of the parameters. By means of (
A.57
) we have:

\displaystyle\bm{H}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})=

\displaystyle\,T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.1}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.2.1}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.2.4}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.3.1}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.3.2}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.3.3}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.3.4}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.4.1}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.4.2}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.4.3}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\right]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.4.4}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.5}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.6.1}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.6.2}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{F\xi}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.6.3}

\displaystyle-T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.6.4}

\displaystyle+T\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{P}^{-1}\otimes\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{\it A.7.1}
