\bm{H}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})
is given by:

\displaystyle\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})=

\displaystyle\,\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\left\{\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\left\{[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{i\cdot}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\left\{\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\left\{\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\otimes\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\otimes[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{i\cdot}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\otimes\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\xi}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\otimes\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{ii}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\left\{\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{\cdot i}\right\}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{\cdot i}\otimes\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right],

(A.88)

where
[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{i\cdot}
and
[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{\cdot i}
are the
i
th row and column of
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}
, respectively, and
[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{ii}
is the
i
th term on its diagonal.
Since,

\displaystyle\left\|\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\|\leq M_{\Lambda},\hskip 9.24994pt\left\|\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\|=O(\sqrt{n}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{C_{\xi}},\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{C_{\xi}},

\displaystyle\left\|\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right\|=\left\|\left\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}^{-1}\right\|=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\right\|\leq\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right\|+\left\|{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{n}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+O(n),

\displaystyle\left\|[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{i\cdot}\right\|=\left\|[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{i\cdot}-[{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{i\cdot}\right\|+\left\|[{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{i\cdot}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+O(\sqrt{n}),

(A.89)

because of Assumption
1
(a), Lemma
2
(i), Assumption
2
(a), Lemma
6
(i), and Lemma
1
(vi), respectively

From (
A.6
) and (
A.89
) it is clear that the leading term in
\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})
is the last one, which is
O_{\mathrm{P}}(T)
while all others are
o_{\mathrm{P}}(T)
, specifically,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\bm{h}^{*}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\;\text{say.}

(A.90)

From (
A.6
)

\displaystyle\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\bm{h}_{ii}^{*\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})\right)=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left\{\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left\{\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right\}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left\{\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right\}\bm{C}_{n,r}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left\{\mathbf{I}_{r}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right).

(A.91)

And, since

\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\bm{h}_{ii}^{*\prime}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}})\right)=\left(\frac{\partial\text{vec}(\bm{h}_{ii}^{*}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}}))^{\prime}}{\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}}\right)^{\prime}\text{vec}\left(\mathrm{d}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\right),

from (
A.91
) and (
A.79
),

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial\text{vec}(\bm{h}_{ii}^{*}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}}))^{\prime}}{\partial\text{vec}(\underline{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}}\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r}

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\right]\bm{C}_{n,r},

(A.92)

which is an
r^{2}\times nr
matrix. From (
A.92
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\text{vec}(\bm{h}^{*}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\underline{\bm{\varphi}}))^{\prime}}{\partial\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{2T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}\,\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{2T}{\underline{\sigma}_{i}^{4}}\left[\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\underline{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}[\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}]_{\cdot i}\otimes\underline{\bm{P}}^{-1}\right].

(A.93)

By using (
A.89
) into (
A.93
) and because of (
A.6
), we have

\left\|\text{vec}\left(\bm{t}_{iii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})\right)\right\|=\left\|\bm{t}_{iii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\bm{\varphi})\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{T}{n}\right).

(A.94)

Moreover, from (
A.85
) it immediately follows that
\text{vec}\left(\bm{T}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})\right)=\mathbf{0}_{n^{3}r^{3}}
.
and, thus,

\text{vec}\left(\bm{t}_{iii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})\right)=\mathbf{0}_{r^{3}}
, or, equivalently,

\bm{t}_{iii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})=\mathbf{0}_{r\times r\times r},

(A.95)

i.e., a 3rd order tensor of zeros.

Finally,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};\widehat{\bm{\varphi}}^{\text{\tiny QML,E}})-\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};\widehat{\bm{\varphi}}^{\text{\tiny QML,E}})\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\,\left\|\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};{\bm{\varphi}})-\bm{h}_{ii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\bm{t}_{iii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}};{\bm{\varphi}})-\bm{t}_{iii}(\bm{\mathcal{X}}|\bm{\mathcal{F}};{\bm{\varphi}})\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML,E}}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\|+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\left\|\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{\tiny QML,E}}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{T}{n},\frac{\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{T}{n}\right)O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right),

which follows from part (b), (
A.94
), (
A.95
), and (
A.4
) in the proof of Corollary
4
, which, in turn, holds even if we use the log-likelihood (
13
) in place of thel log-likelihood (
12
). This completes the proof.
\Box

Appendix B
Auxiliary propositions

Proposition B.1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
5
,

(a)

\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
;

(b)

\bm{\Lambda}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof of Proposition
B.1
.
For part (a), first notice that Assumptions
1
(c) and
5
(b), imply
\bm{\Gamma}^{F}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
which, in turn, implies
\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}=\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}
. Therefore, since the non-zero eigenvalues of
\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}}{n}
are the same as the
r
eigenvalues of
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}
, which is diagonal by Assumption
5
(a). Then, we must have, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}
. Equivalently, for a given
T
, from Assumption
5
(b), we have
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\chi}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\chi}_{t}\bm{\chi}_{t}^{\prime}=\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}
which implies that
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}
. However, since
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\chi}={\bm{\Gamma}}^{\chi}
it must be that
\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}=\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}
. This proves part (a).

For part (b), since
\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}
, it must be that

\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2},

(B.1)

for some
r\times r
invertible
\mathbf{K}_{*}
. Now, since
\text{rk}(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}})=r
for all
n>N
(see the proof of Proposition
B.2
):

\mathbf{K}_{*}=(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2},

(B.2)

which is also obtained by linear projection, and

\mathbf{K}^{-1}_{*}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1/2}{\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}.

(B.3)

Notice that since
\mathbf{K}_{*}
is a special case of the matrix
\mathbf{K}
defined in (
B.10
) in the proof of Proposition
B.2
, then
\mathbf{K}_{*}

is finite and positive definite because of Lemma
10
(i) and
10
(ii), respectively, hence,
\mathbf{K}^{-1}_{*}
in (
B.3
) is well defined.

Moreover, from (
B.2
), because of Assumption
5
(b) and part (a):

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{*}\mathbf{K}_{*}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\bm{\Lambda}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}_{r}.

(B.4)

So because of (
B.4
), we have that
\mathbf{K}_{*}
is an orthogonal matrix, i.e.,
\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}
.
Finally, by (
B.2
) we also have

\mathbf{V}^{\chi}=\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1/2}

(B.5)

and by substituting (
B.5
) into (
B.3
), because of (
B.4
),

\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{*}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1}\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda},

which is equivalent to:

\mathbf{K}_{*}^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{M}^{\chi},

(B.6)

and by part (a), we must have
\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. Alternatively, by multiplying on the right both sides of (
B.1
) by their transposed:

\mathbf{K}_{*}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{M}^{\chi},

(B.7)

since eigenvectors are normalized, and again by part (a), we must have
\mathbf{K}_{*}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. So from (
B.1
) or (
B.7
) we prove part (b). This completes the proof.
\Box

Proposition B.2
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, as
n,T\to\infty

\min(n,\sqrt{T})\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
,
where
\bm{\mathcal{H}}=(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}\mathbf{J}

and
\mathbf{J}
is an
r\times r
diagonal matrix with entries
\pm 1
.

Proof.

Notice that
\text{rk}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=r
for all
n
, since
\text{rk}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})=r
by Assumption
1
(b) and
\text{rk}\left(\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}}{n}\right)=r
by Lemma
1
(iv).
Indeed,
\text{rk}\left(\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}}{n}\right)\leq\min(\text{rk}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F}),\text{rk}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right))
.
This holds for all
n>N
and since eigenvalues are an increasing sequence in
n
. Therefore,
(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n})^{-1}
is well defined for all
n
and
\bm{\Lambda}
admits a left inverse.

Second, since

\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}}{n}=\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}=\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(B.8)

the columns of
\frac{\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}
and the columns of
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}
must span the same space.

So there exists an
r\times r
invertible matrix
\mathbf{K}
such that

{\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}}\mathbf{K}={\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}}

(B.9)

Therefore, from (
B.9
)

\mathbf{K}=(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}

(B.10)

which is also obtained by linear projection, and also

\mathbf{K}^{-1}=(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1/2}{\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}

(B.11)

which are both finite and positive definite because of Lemma
10
.

Now, from (
B.9
) and (
B.10
)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}^{\chi}({\mathbf{M}}^{\chi})^{1/2}=\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\mathbf{K}=\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}

(B.12)

Let

\bm{\mathcal{H}}=(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}\mathbf{J},

(B.13)

which is finite and positive definite because of Lemma
11
.

Now,
because of Lemmas
6
(iii),
6
(iv),
8
(i), using (
9
), (
B.12
), and (
B.13
),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|=

\displaystyle\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}-{\mathbf{V}}^{\chi}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\mathbf{J}\right\|=\ \left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{1/2}-{\mathbf{V}}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\left(\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-{\mathbf{V}}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\{\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\right)^{1/2}\right\}\right\|\,\left\|\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-{\mathbf{V}}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\{\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\right)^{1/2}\right\}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right),

since
\|\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\|=1
because eigenvectors are normalized.
This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition B.3
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
the terms in (
A.5
) are such that, as
n,T\to\infty
,

(a)

\sqrt{nT}\left\|\text{(1.a)}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(b)

\sqrt{T}\left\|\text{(1.b)}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(c)

\min(n,\sqrt{nT})\left\|\text{(1.c)}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(d)

\min(\sqrt{nT},T)\left\|\text{(1.d)}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(e)

\min(\sqrt{nT},T)\left\|\text{(1.e)}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(f)

\min(n,\sqrt{nT},T)\left\|\text{(1.f)}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

uniformly in
i
.

Proof.

For part (a), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, by Assumption
1
(a),

\left\|\frac{1}{nT}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|\leq\left\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|\leq M_{\Lambda}\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|.

(B.14)

Then, by Assumptions
1
(a) and
3

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{n^{2}T^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{h=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{kt}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{jt}[\bm{\Lambda}_{jh}]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r^{2}}{n^{2}T^{2}}\max_{h,k=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[F_{kt}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{jt}[\bm{\Lambda}_{jh}]\right)F_{ks}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\xi_{\ell s}[\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell h}]\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}}{n^{2}T^{2}}\max_{h,k=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{kt}F_{ks}]\,\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{jt}\xi_{\ell s}\right][\bm{\Lambda}_{jh}][\bm{\Lambda}_{\ell h}]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{r^{2}}{T}\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[F_{kt}F_{ks}\right]\right\}\,\left\{\max_{t,s=1\ldots,T}\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{2}}{n^{2}T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{jt}\xi_{\ell s}]\right|\right\}.

(B.15)

Now, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, for any
k=1,\ldots,r
,

\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}F_{kt}F_{ks}\right|\leq\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{kt}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}F_{ks}^{2}\right)^{1/2},

(B.16)

and, by Assumption
1
(b), using (
B.16
) and again Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{kt}F_{ks}]

\displaystyle=\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}F_{kt}F_{ks}\right]\leq\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}F_{kt}F_{ks}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{kt}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}F_{ks}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{kt}^{2}\right)\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}F_{ks}^{2}\right)\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{kt}^{2}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{ks}^{2}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{kt}^{2}]\leq\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}[F_{kt}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\max_{k=1,\ldots,r}\bm{\eta}_{k}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}_{F}\bm{\eta}_{k}\leq\|\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\|\leq M_{F},

(B.17)

since
M_{F}
is independent of
t
and where
\bm{\eta}_{k}
is an
r
-dimensional vector with one in the
k
th entry and zero elsewhere. And, because of Lemma
1
(ii)

\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,T}\frac{1}{n^{2}T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{jt}\xi_{\ell s}]\right|\leq\frac{M_{\xi}}{nT},

(B.18)

since
M_{\xi}
is independent of
n
and
t
.
By substituting (
B.17
) and (
B.18
) into (
B.15
),

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{r^{2}M_{F}M_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{\xi}}{nT}.

(B.19)

By substituting (
B.19
) into (
B.14
), we prove part (a).

For part (b), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, because of Lemma
2
(i),

\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bm{\lambda}_{j}\bm{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|=\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\leq\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}\right\|M_{\Lambda}^{2}.

(B.20)

Then, by Assumptions
3
and
2
(b) and using (
B.17
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}F_{jt}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{T^{2}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}F_{jt}\xi_{is}F_{js}]=\frac{r}{T^{2}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]\,\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{r}{T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq rM_{F}\frac{M_{ii}}{T}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}\rho^{|t-s|}\leq\frac{rM_{F}M_{\xi}}{T},

(B.21)

since
M_{\xi}
is independent of
i
. Or, equivalently, by Lemma
1
(iii) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{T^{2}}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]\,\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{r}{T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}|\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]|\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}^{2}]\frac{M_{\xi}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}\leq\frac{rM_{F}M_{3\xi}}{T},

(B.22)

since
M_{F}
is independent of
t
and
M_{3\xi}
is independent of
i
. Notice that
M_{\xi}\leq M_{3\xi}
. Notice that (
B.22
) is a special case of Lemma
3
(i). By substituting (
B.21
), or (
B.22
), into (
B.20
), we prove part (b).

For part (c), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, because of Assumption
1
(a),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}\bm{\lambda}_{j}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}\lambda_{jk}\right)^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{r}M_{\Lambda}\left|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r}M_{\Lambda}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right|+\left|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right|\right\}.

(B.23)

Then, by Assumption
2
(b),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right|\leq\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right|\leq\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right|\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}M_{ij}\leq\frac{M_{\xi}}{n},

(B.24)

since
M_{\xi}
is independent of
i
and
t
. Moreover, by Assumption
2
(c),

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K_{\xi}}{nT}.

(B.25)

By substituting (
B.24
) and (
B.25
) into (
B.23
), we prove part (c).

For part (d), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, because of Assumption
1
(a)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{nT}{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\mathcal{H}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{\Lambda}\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{F}_{t}\xi_{jt}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\mathcal{H}})\right\|

\displaystyle=M_{\Lambda}\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}})}{nT}\right\|\leq M_{\Lambda}\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|.

(B.26)

Then, by using Lemma
3
and Proposition
B.2
in (
B.26
), we prove part (d).

For part (e), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bm{\lambda}_{j}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\mathcal{H}})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\mathbf{F}_{t}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|.

(B.27)

By substituting part (ii), Lemma
2
(i), and part (a) of Proposition
B.2
into (
B.27
), we prove part (e).

Finally, for part (f), for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, let
\bm{\zeta}_{i}=(\xi_{i1}\cdots\xi_{iT})^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}(\widehat{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\mathcal{H}})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right)}{nT}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|.

(B.28)

Then, by the
C_{r}
-inequality with
r=2
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right)^{2}\right\}

(B.29)

By taking the expectation of (
B.29
), and because of Assumption
2
(c) and Lemma
1
(v),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{2}{nT^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{is}\xi_{js}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K_{\xi}}{T}+\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right|\max_{s=1,\ldots,T}\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{is}\xi_{js}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{ij}\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,n}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\leq\frac{2K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{2M_{\xi}}{n}\|\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\|\leq\frac{2K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{2M_{\xi}M_{2\xi}}{n},

(B.30)

since
K_{\xi}
is independent of
j
and
M_{\xi}
is independent of
i
,
j
,
t
, and
s
and where
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}
is an
n
-dimensional vector with one in the
i
th entry and zero elsewhere.
By substituting (
B.30
) and part (a) of Proposition
B.2
into (
B.28
), we prove part (f). This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition B.4
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
,

(a)

\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)-\bm{Q}_{0}\right\|=o(1)
, as
n\to\infty
;

(b)

\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)-\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)\right\|=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
, as
n,T\to\infty
.

where

\bm{Q}_{0}=\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\mathcal{J}}_{0}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}^{\prime}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}
, and where

\bm{\mathcal{J}}_{0}
is an
r\times r
diagonal matrix with entries
\pm 1
,
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}
is the
r\times r
matrix having as columns the normalized eigenvectors of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
, and
\bm{V}_{0}
is the
r\times r
matrix of corresponding eigenvalues sorted in descending order.

Proof.

Start with part (a). From (
B.13
) and (
B.11
)

\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)=\mathbf{J}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)=\mathbf{J}\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}=\mathbf{J}\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)\mathbf{K}^{-1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}.

(B.31)

Then, from (
B.10
)

\mathbf{V}^{\chi}=\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\mathbf{K}({\mathbf{M}^{\chi}})^{-1/2}

(B.32)

thus, from (
B.32
) and (
B.11
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)\mathbf{K}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{J}\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{-1/2}{\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{J}\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)({\mathbf{M}^{\chi}})^{-1}\mathbf{K}^{\prime}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{J}\mathbf{K}^{\prime}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}.

(B.33)

And, from (
B.10
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{K}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{J}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\chi}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}(\mathbf{M}^{\chi})^{1/2}\mathbf{V}^{\chi\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}\mathbf{J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{J}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}\mathbf{J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{J}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})^{-1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}\mathbf{J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{J}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}\mathbf{J}=\mathbf{I}_{r}.

(B.34)

Therefore, the columns of
\mathbf{J}\mathbf{K}
are the normalized eigenvectors of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
with eigenvalues
\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}
(notice that
\mathbf{J}\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)=\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)\mathbf{J}
). Moreover, by Assumption
1
(a)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}-(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\right\|=0.

(B.35)

Letting,
\bm{V}_{0}
be the matrix of eigenvalues of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
sorted in descending order, from (
B.35
) we also have (this is proved also in Lemma
9
(i))

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\right\|=0.

(B.36)

Let
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}
be the normalized eigenvectors of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}

Hence, by continuity of eigenvectors, and since the eigenvalues
\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}
are distinct because of Assumption
4
, from
Yu
et al. 2015
, by Lemma
9
(iii) and using (
B.35
) it follows that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\mathbf{J}\mathbf{K}-\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}\bm{\mathcal{J}}_{0}\right\|\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{2^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\left\|(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}-(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\right\|}{\mu_{r}(\bm{V}_{0})}=0,

(B.37)

where
\bm{\mathcal{J}}_{0}
is an
r\times r
diagonal matrix with entries
\pm 1
, which is in general different from
\mathbf{J}
. Finally, since
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}
is an orthogonal matrix, we have
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}^{-1}=\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}^{\prime}
, so from (
B.37
)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\mathbf{K}^{-1}\mathbf{J}-\bm{\mathcal{J}}_{0}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}^{\prime}\right\|=0.

(B.38)

By using (
B.38
) and (
B.36
) into (
B.31
) and since
\mathbf{K}^{-1}\mathbf{J}=\mathbf{J}\mathbf{K}^{-1}
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right)-\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\mathcal{J}}_{0}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}^{\prime}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}\right\|=0.

By defining
\bm{Q}_{0}=\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\mathcal{J}}_{0}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}^{\prime}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{-1/2}
, we prove part (a).

Part (b) follows from Proposition
B.2
and Lemma
2
(i), and since

\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}-\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}-\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(B.39)

This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition B.5
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, as
n,T\to\infty
,

\min(n,\sqrt{nT},T)\left\|\frac{(\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
, where
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
is defined in (
A.3
).

Proof.

We have,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{(\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\left\|\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}})^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}})^{\prime}(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}})}{n}\right\|\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{I}+\bm{II}\right\},\;\text{say.}

(B.40)

First, consider
\bm{I}
in (
B.40
). From (
A.4
)

\displaystyle\bm{I}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}})^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{nT}+\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{nT}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{n}+\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{n^{2}T}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}})^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}}{nT}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}})^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}})^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}=\bm{I}_{a}+\bm{I}_{b}+\bm{I}_{c}+\bm{I}_{d}+\bm{I}_{e}+\bm{I}_{f},\;\text{say.}

(B.41)

Then, because of (
B.15
) and (
B.19
) in the proof of Proposition
B.3
(a),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}}{nT}\right\|^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{n^{2}T^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{h=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{kt}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{jt}\lambda_{jh}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{r^{2}M_{F}M_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{\xi}}{nT}.

(B.42)

Therefore, by Assumption
1
(a), Lemma (
8
)(iv),
11
(i),
12
(i),
and using (
B.42
) , we get

\displaystyle\|\bm{I}_{a}\|\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}\bm{\Lambda}}{nT}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right),

(B.43)

\displaystyle\|\bm{I}_{b}\|\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{nT}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right).

(B.44)

Moreover, because of Lemma
2
(i),
3
(iii),
8
(iv),
12
(i), and
11
(i),

\displaystyle\|\bm{I}_{c}\|\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\right)\right).

(B.45)

Similarly, because of Proposition
B.2
(a), Lemma
2
(i),
3
(i),
3
(iv),
8
(iv), and
12
(i),

\displaystyle\|\bm{I}_{d}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right),

(B.46)

\displaystyle\|\bm{I}_{e}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{F}\bm{}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right),

(B.47)

and, because of Proposition
B.2
(a), Lemma
2
(i),
3
(iii),
8
(iv), and
12
(i),

\displaystyle\|\bm{I}_{f}\|\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{n\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.48)

By using (
B.43
), (
B.44
), (
B.45
), (
B.46
), (
B.47
), and (
B.48
) into (
B.41
)

\|\bm{I}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.49)

Second, consider
\bm{II}
in (
B.40
). From Proposition
(b),

\|\bm{II}\|\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-{\bm{\Lambda}}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|^{2}=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.50)

And, by using (
B.49
) and (
B.50
) in (
B.40
), because of Lemma
2
(ii), and Lemma
12
(i), we complete the proof.
∎

Proposition B.6
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, and Assumption
5
, if
\sqrt{T}/n\to 0
and
\sqrt{n}/T\to 0
, as
n,T\to\infty
,

\min(\sqrt{n},\sqrt{T})\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}-\bm{J}\|=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;
where
\bm{J}
is a diagonal
r\times r
matrix with entries
\pm 1
.

Proof.

From Proposition
B.5
and by using (
9
)

\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}{n}=\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}(\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}+\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}})}{n}=\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.51)

Or, equivalently,

\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.52)

Moreover, by Assumption
5
and using (
B.52
) in (
A.3
) we have

\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.53)

Therefore, because of (
B.53
), as
n,T\to\infty
,
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
is an
r\times r
orthogonal matrix thus it has eigenvalues
\pm 1
.

Moreover, because of Proposition
B.5

\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}+\bm{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathbf{H}})^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.54)

Thus, from (
B.53
) and (
B.54
) and by Lemma
8
(iv),

\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}=\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.55)

And from (
B.55
) it follows that

\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

(B.56)

So, because of (
B.56
), as
n,T\to\infty
, the columns of
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
are the eigenvectors of
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}
with eigenvalues
\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}
. The eigenvectors are normalized since
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
is orthogonal, as
n,T\to\infty
. Moreover, under Assumption
5
,
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}
is diagonal, so, as
n,T\to\infty
,
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
must be diagonal with eigenvalues
\pm 1
. By noticing that, if
\sqrt{T}/n\to 0
and
\sqrt{n}/T\to 0
, then
\max\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)=o_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right)
, we complete the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Auxiliary lemmata

Lemma 1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
:

(i)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{js}]|\leq M_{1\xi}
, for some finite positive real
M_{1\xi}
independent of
n
and
T
;

(ii)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]|\leq M_{2\xi}
, for some finite positive real
M_{2\xi}
independent of
n
and
t
;

(iii)

for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\!\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]|\leq M_{3\xi}
, for some finite positive real
M_{3\xi}
independent of
i
and
T
;

(iv)

for all
j=1,\ldots,r
,
\underline{C}_{j}\!\leq\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\!\overline{C}_{j}
,
for some finite positive reals
\underline{C}_{j}
and
\overline{C}_{j}
;

(v)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mu_{1}^{\xi}\leq M_{2\xi}
, where
M_{\xi}
is defined in part (ii);

(vi)

for all
j=1,\ldots,r
,
\underline{C}_{j}\leq\lim\inf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{x}}{n}\leq\lim\sup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}^{x}}{n}\leq\overline{C}_{j}
, and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mu_{r+1}^{x}\leq M_{\xi}
, where
M_{\xi}
is defined in Assumption
2
(b).

Proof.

Using Assumptions
2
(a) and
2
(b), we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{js}]|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=-(T-1)}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{|k|}{T}\right)|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{j,t-k}]|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\rho^{|k|}\sigma_{i}^{2}+\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\rho^{|k|}M_{ij}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\xi}^{\prime}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}+\frac{M_{\xi}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}^{2}+\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}M_{ij}\leq C_{\xi}^{\prime}+M_{\xi},

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]|

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=-(T-1)}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{|k|}{T}\right)|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{i,t-k}]|\leq\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\rho^{|k|}\sigma_{i}^{2}\leq\frac{C_{\xi}^{\prime}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}.

Defining,
M_{1\xi}=\frac{(C_{\xi}^{\prime}+M_{\xi})(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}
and
M_{2\xi}=C_{\xi}^{\prime}+M_{\xi}
, and
M_{3\xi}=\frac{C_{\xi}^{\prime}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}
, we prove parts (i), (ii), and (iii).

For part (iv), by
Merikoski and
Kumar 2004
, for all
j=1,\ldots,r
, we have

\frac{\mu_{r}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})}{n}\mu_{j}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})\leq\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\frac{\mu_{j}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})}{n}\ \mu_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F}).

(C.1)

The proof then follows from Assumption
1
(a) which, by continuity of eigenvalues, implies that, for any
j=1,\ldots,r
, as
n\to\infty

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})}{n}=\mu_{j}(\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}).

with

0<m_{\Lambda}^{2}\leq\mu_{r}(\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda})\leq\mu_{1}(\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda})\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}<\infty,

and by Assumption
1
(b) and Assumption
1
(c) which imply

0<m_{F}\leq\mu_{r}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})\leq\mu_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})\leq M_{F}<\infty.

For part (v), by Assumption
2
(b):

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\|\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]|\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sigma_{i}^{2}+\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}M_{ij}\leq C_{\xi}^{\prime}+M_{\xi}.

Part (vi) follows from parts (iv) and (v) and Weyl’s inequality. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, for all
t=1,\ldots,T
and all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}

(i)

\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|\mathbf{F}_{t}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
and
\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.

By Assumption
1
(a), which holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}_{F}=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{ij}^{2}\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|^{2}\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2},

since
M_{\Lambda}
is independent of
i
. This proves part (i).

By Assumption
1
(b), which holds for all
T\in\mathbb{N}
because of stationarity (see also part (i) of Lemma
6
),

\displaystyle\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{F}_{t}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}^{2}]\leq r\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq r\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\bm{\eta}_{j}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\bm{\eta}_{j}\leq r\|\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\|\leq rM_{F},

(C.2)

since
M_{F}
is independent of
t
and where
\bm{\eta}_{j}
is an
r
-dimensional vector with one in the
j
th entry and zero elsewhere. Therefore, from (
C
):

\displaystyle\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[[\bm{F}]_{tj}^{2}]\leq\sup_{T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\ldots,T}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{F}_{t}\|^{2}]\leq rM_{F}.

This proves part (ii) and it completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}

\sqrt{T}\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.

By Assumption
3
, Lemma
1
(iii) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{\sqrt{n}T}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nT^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{jt}\xi_{it}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{T^{2}}\max_{j=1\ldots,r}\max_{i=1\ldots,n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}F_{jt}\xi_{is}F_{js}]

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{T^{2}}\max_{j=1\ldots,r}\max_{i=1\ldots,n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]\,\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{r}{T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}|\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{js}]|\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left|\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{is}]\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,r}\max_{t,s=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}^{2}]\frac{M_{\xi}(1+\rho)}{1-\rho}\leq\frac{rM_{F}M_{3\xi}}{T},

since
M_{F}
is independent of
t
and
s
and
M_{3\xi}
is independent of
i
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}

(i)

\min(n,\sqrt{T})\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

\min(n,\sqrt{nT})\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iii)

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|=O(1)
.

Proof.

For part (i), first notice that, by Lemmas
1
(v) and
5
(ii)

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|=\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|+\frac{\mu_{1}^{\xi}}{n}=O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(C.3)

Similarly, for part (ii) by Lemmas
2
(i) and
1
(v)

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|=\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(C.4)

Then, because of Assumption
2
(c),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{n^{3/2}T}-\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n^{3/2}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n^{3/2}T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\lambda_{ik}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\lambda_{ik}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}n}{n^{2}T}\max_{j=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}]\right\}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}K_{\xi}}{nT},

(C.5)

since
K_{\xi}
is independent of
j
. By using (
C.5
) into (
C.4
),
we prove part (ii).

Part (iii) follows from Lemma
2
(i) since

\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5
.

(i)

Under Assumption
1
, for all
T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sqrt{T}\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

Under Assumption
2
, for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sqrt{T}\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.

Part (i) is direct consequence of Assumption
1
(c-ii), since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{F_{jt}F_{kt}-\mathbb{E}[F_{jt}F_{kt}]\right\}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{r^{2}C_{F}}{T},

since
C_{F}
is independent of
j
and
k
.

For part (ii), by Assumption
2
(c), letting
\gamma_{ij}^{\xi}
be the
(i,j)
the entry of
\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{\Xi}^{\prime}\bm{\Xi}}{nT}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right\}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}T^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{\xi}\right\}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,n}\frac{1}{T^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-\gamma_{ij}^{\xi}\right\}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K_{\xi}}{T},

since
K_{\xi}
is independent of
i
and
j
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
,
for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}

(i)

\frac{\sqrt{T}}{n}\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

\frac{\min(n,\sqrt{T})}{n}\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iii)

\frac{\min(n,\sqrt{T})}{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(iv)

as
n\to\infty
,
{\min(n,\sqrt{T})}\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
, where
\mathbf{J}
is
r\times r
diagonal with entries
\pm 1
.

Proof.

For part (i), from Lemma
5
(i),
5
(ii) and
2
(i):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left\{\bm{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right\}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left\{\bm{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\right\}\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{4}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}r^{2}C_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}.

This proves part (i).

Part (ii), follows from

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]+\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{n}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{\mu_{1}^{\xi}}{n^{2}}\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{M_{2\xi}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{T}\right),\;\text{say,}

because of part (i) and Lemma
1
(v) and where
M_{1}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
.

For part (iii), for any
j=1,\ldots,r
, because of Weyl’s inequality and part (ii), it holds that

|\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{x}-\mu_{j}^{\chi}|\leq\mu_{1}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})=\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right\|.

(C.6)

Hence, from (
C.6
) and part (ii),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}-\mathbf{M}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{x}-\mu_{j}^{\chi}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{x}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}\right)^{2}\right]\leq r\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{rM_{\Lambda}^{4}K_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{rM_{\xi}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq rM_{1}\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}},\frac{1}{T}\right).

(C.7)

This proves part (iii).

For part (iv), because of
Yu
et al. 2015
, which is a special case of Davis Kahn Theorem, there exists an
r\times r
diagonal matrix
\mathbf{J}
with entries
\pm 1
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\|\leq\frac{2^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right\|}{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)},

(C.8)

where
\mu_{0}^{\chi}=\infty
. This holds provided the eigenvalues
\mu_{j}^{\chi}
are distinct as required by Assumption
4
.
Therefore, from (
C.8
), part (ii) and Lemma
1
(iv), and since
\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}=0
, as
n\to\infty

\displaystyle\min(n^{2},T)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\min(n^{2},T)2^{3}\frac{r}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right\|^{2}\right]}{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)\right\}^{2}}\leq\frac{8rM_{1}}{\underline{C}_{r}^{2}}=M_{2},\;\text{say,}

where
M_{2}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
.
This proves part (iv). This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7
.

Let
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}
be the
n
-dimensional vector with one in entry
i
and zero elsewhere. Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, for all
i=1,\ldots,n
and
T\in\mathbb{N}
and as
n\to\infty

(i)

\frac{\min(\sqrt{n},\sqrt{T})}{\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
;

(ii)

\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|=O(1)
;

(iii)

{\min(\sqrt{n},\sqrt{T})}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}^{x\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{v}^{\chi\prime}\mathbf{J}\|=O_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
.

Proof.

First notice that

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-[\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}]_{ij}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\xi_{jt}-[\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}]_{ij}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K_{\xi}}{T},

(C.9)

since
K_{\xi}
is independent of
i
and
j
. Therefore, from Lemma
5
(i) and Lemma
2
(i), and using (
C.9
),

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\,\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\{\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right)\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{F}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{t}\bm{\xi}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}r^{2}C_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T},

(C.10)

since
M_{\Lambda}
and
C_{F}
are independent of
i
. Then, following the same arguments as Lemma
6
(ii), because of (
C.10
) and Lemma
1
(v):

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right]+\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{i}\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}r^{2}C_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}r^{2}C_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{\mu_{1}^{\xi}}{n}\leq\frac{M_{\Lambda}^{4}r^{2}C_{F}+K_{\xi}}{T}+\frac{M_{2\xi}}{n}\leq M_{1}\max\left(\frac{1}{T},\frac{1}{n}\right),\;\text{say,}

since
\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\|=1
and where
M_{1}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
defined in Lemma
6
(ii). This proves part (i).

For part (ii), notice that for all
i=1,\ldots,n
we must have:

\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}(\chi_{it})=\bm{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\bm{\lambda}_{i}\leq\|\bm{\lambda}_{i}\|^{2}\,\left\|\bm{\Gamma}^{F}\right\|\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{F},

(C.11)

which is finite for all
i
and
t
. So, since by Lemma
1
(iv)

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ar}(\chi_{it})=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mu_{j}^{\chi}[\mathbf{V}^{\chi}]_{ij}^{2}\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mu_{r}^{\chi}\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}[\mathbf{V}^{\chi}]_{ij}^{2}\geq\underline{C}_{r}n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|^{2},

then, because of (
C.11
), we must have, as
n\to\infty
,

\underline{C}_{r}n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|^{2}\leq M_{\Lambda}^{2}M_{F}

which implies that, as
n\to\infty
,

n\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi}\|^{2}\leq M_{V},

for some finite positive real
M_{V}
independent of
n
. This proves part (ii).

Finally, using the same arguments in Lemma
6
(iv), from (
C.8
), part (i) and Lemma
1
(iv), and since
\mu_{0}^{\chi}=\infty
and
\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}=0
, as
n\to\infty

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\min(n,T)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{x\prime}-\mathbf{v}_{i}^{\chi\prime}\mathbf{J})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\min(n,T)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\sqrt{n}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{x}-\mathbf{V}^{\chi}\mathbf{J})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\ \frac{\min(n,T)2^{3}\frac{r}{n^{2}}n\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})\right\|^{2}\right]}{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)\right\}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\ \frac{\min(n,T)2^{3}\frac{r}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{x}-\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi})\right\|^{2}\right]}{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\{\min(|\mu_{0}^{\chi}-\mu_{1}^{\chi}|,|\mu_{r}^{\chi}-\mu_{r+1}^{\chi}|)\right\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8rM_{1}}{\underline{C}_{r}^{2}}=M_{2},

where
M_{2}
is a finite positive real independent of
n
and
T
defined in Lemma
6
(iv).
This proves part (iii) and completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, for all
n,T\in\mathbb{N}
,

(i)

\|\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|(\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n})^{-1}\|=O(1)
;

(iii)

\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
;

(iv)

\|(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n})^{-1}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
.

Proof.

Parts (i) and (ii) follow directly from Lemma
1
(iv), indeed,

\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right\|=\frac{\mu_{1}^{\chi}}{n}\leq\overline{C}_{1},

and

\left\|\left(\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|=\frac{n}{\mu_{r}^{\chi}}\leq\frac{1}{\underline{C}_{r}}.

Both statements hold for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
since the eigenvalues are an increasing sequence in
n
.

For part (iii), because of part (i) and Lemma
6
(iii),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}-\frac{{\mathbf{M}}^{\chi}}{n}\right\|\leq\overline{C}_{1}+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right).

For part (iv) just notice that, because of Lemma
6
(iii) and part (ii), then
\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}
is positive definite with probability tending to one as
n,T\to\infty
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
,

(i)

\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\|=o(1)
, as
n\to\infty
and
\|\bm{V}_{0}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\|=o_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
, as
n,T\to\infty
;

(iii)

\|(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n})^{-1}-\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\|=o(1)
, as
n\to\infty
and
\|\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\|=O(1)

(iv)

\|(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n})^{-1}-\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\|=o_{\mathrm{ms}}(1)
, as
n,T\to\infty
,

where
\bm{V}_{0}
is
r\times r
diagonal with entries the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}\bm{\Gamma}^{F}
sorted in descending order.

Proof.

For part (i), first notice that the
r
non-zero eigenvalues of
\frac{\bm{\Gamma}^{\chi}}{n}
are also the
r
eigenvalues of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
which in turn are also the entries of
\bm{V}_{0}
. The proof follows from continuity of eigenvalues and since, because of Assumption
1
(a), as
n\to\infty
,

\left\|(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}}{n}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}-(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\right\|=o(1).

Part (ii) is a consequence of part (i) and Lemma
8
(iii).

For part (iii) notice that

\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{-1}-\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\right\|\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\mathbf{M}^{\chi}}{n}-\bm{V}_{0}\right\|\,\left\|\bm{V}_{0}^{-1}\right\|,

then the proof follows from part (i), Lemma
8
(ii), and since
\bm{V}_{0}
is positive definite since
\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{F}
are positive definite by Assumptions
1
(a) and
1
(b), respectively. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 10
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, as
n\to\infty
,

(i)

\|{\mathbf{K}}\|=O(1)
;

(ii)

\|{\mathbf{K}}^{-1}\|=O(1)
.

Proof.

For part (a), from (
B.37
) in the proof of Proposition
B.4
(a) it follows that

\left\|\mathbf{K}-\mathbf{J}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}\right\|=o(1),

(C.12)

where
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}
is the
r\times r
matrix of normalized eigenvectors of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
and
\mathbf{J}
is a diagonal matrix with entries
\pm 1
. Part (i) follows from the fact that
\|\mathbf{J}\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}\|=O(1)
, since
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
is finite. Likewise part (ii) follows from the fact that
\mathbf{J}
is obviously positive definite and
\bm{\Upsilon}_{0}
is also positive definite because the eigenvalues of
(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\Lambda}(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}
are distinct by Assumption
4
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, as
n\to\infty
,

\|{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}\|=O(1)
.

Proof.

From (
B.12
) and (
B.13
) in the proof of Proposition
B.2

\bm{\mathcal{H}}=(\bm{\Gamma}^{F})^{1/2}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{J}
. Then, the proof follows immediately from Assumption
1
(b), Lemma
10
(i), and since
\mathbf{J}
is obviously finite and positive definite.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
, as
n,T\to\infty
,

\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\|=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
.

Proof.

From (
A.3
), by Proposition
B.2
(a), Lemma
3
(v),
8
(iv)
11
(i), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{F}^{\prime}\bm{F}}{T}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{n}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\bm{\mathcal{H}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bm{F}}{\sqrt{T}}\right\|^{2}\,\left\|\frac{\bm{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}-\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\|\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{M}}^{x}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)+O_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Under Assumptions
1
through
3
and letting
\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi}
be the
n\times n
diagonal matrix with diagonal entries the diagonal entries of
\bm{\Gamma}^{\xi}
, as
n\to\infty
,

n^{-1}\left\|\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\left\{{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\right\}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|=O\left(1\right).

Proof.

First notice that for any two invertible matrices
\bm{K}
and
\bm{H}
we have

\displaystyle(\bm{H}+\bm{K})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\bm{H}+\bm{K})^{-1}-\bm{K}^{-1}+\bm{K}^{-1}=(\bm{H}+\bm{K})^{-1}(\bm{K}-(\bm{H}+\bm{K}))\bm{K}^{-1}+\bm{K}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\bm{H}+\bm{K})^{-1}(-\bm{H})\bm{K}^{-1}+\bm{K}^{-1}=\bm{K}^{-1}-(\bm{H}+\bm{K})^{-1}\bm{H}\bm{K}^{-1}.

(C.13)

Then, setting
\bm{K}=\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}
and
\bm{H}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
from (
C.13
) we have

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}=\{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\}^{-1}-\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\}^{-1}.

(C.14)

which implies

\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\}=\mathbf{I}_{r}-\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}.

(C.15)

Then, by Weyl’s inequality:

\nu_{r}(\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}})\geq 1+\nu_{r}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}})\geq\nu_{r}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}).

(C.16)

From, (
C.16
), we have

\displaystyle\|\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu_{r}(\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}})}\leq\frac{1}{\nu_{r}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}})}.

(C.17)

Now, the
r
eigenvalues of
{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}
are also the
r
non-zero eigenvalues of
\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{(}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}
, and the
r
non-zero eigenvalues of
\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}
are the
r
eigenvalues of
\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda}
.
Therefore, because of
Merikoski and
Kumar 2004
and Proposition
B.1
(a), and Lemma
1
(iv):

\displaystyle\nu_{r}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}})

\displaystyle\geq\nu_{r}(\bm{\Lambda}\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime})\nu_{r}(({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1})=\frac{\nu_{r}(\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}\bm{\Lambda})}{\nu_{1}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})}=\frac{\mu_{r}^{\chi}}{\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sigma_{i}^{2}}\geq\frac{n\underline{C}_{r}}{C_{\xi}}.

(C.18)

By substituting (
C.18
) into (
C.17
) we have

\|\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{C_{\xi}}{n\underline{C}_{r}}.

(C.19)

Therefore, from (
C.15
) and (
C.19
),

\left\|\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\{\bm{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\xi})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}\}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|=\left\|\{\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\xi})^{-1}{\bm{\Lambda}}\}^{-1}\right\|\leq\frac{C_{\xi}}{n\underline{C}_{r}},

which completes the proof.
∎
